But honestly, I haven't really noticed it too much outside of those long situations at work.\n\nDoctor: When you’re not able to sit still, do you also find yourself blurting things out or interrupting other people?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It happens a lot at work, actually. I'll often jump in with an answer before a client or one of my coworkers has even finished asking their question. And then... well, I usually realize I just answered the wrong thing because I didn't wait to hear the whole question, or I just realize I completely cut them off. It's definitely something I catch myself doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you often gotten into fights, threatened people, or deliberately broken rules or property?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. I mean, I can be impatient and interrupt people, like I said, but I don't get into fights or threaten anyone. And I definitely don't go around breaking things or deliberately breaking rules. That's just not me.\n\nDoctor: Before age 12, did you also tend to be unusually fidgety or unable to stay seated when expected?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely did. I remember this all starting way before high school, honestly. My elementary school teachers used to write on my report cards that I was bright but always rushed through things. They'd say I talked out of turn a lot, forgot my homework... and my desk was always just stuffed with half-finished papers. So, yeah, I've had a hard time sitting still and focusing for a long time.\n\nDoctor: Besides the interrupting and trouble sitting still, do you also often feel overly active or like you have to keep moving?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel overly active like that. I mean, I get restless, sure, but I don't feel like I have a motor running inside me or anything where I just have to keep moving constantly. It's more just the fidgeting and having a hard time staying put in one spot for too long.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I don't know if I'd call it a constant restlessness, but... sitting still for a long time just gets really uncomfortable. I just get this urge that I need to move or stretch my legs. But honestly, I haven't really noticed it too much outside of those long situations at work.\n\nDoctor: When you’re not able to sit still, do you also find yourself blurting things out or interrupting other people?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It happens a lot at work, actually. I'll often jump in with an answer before a client or one of my coworkers has even finished asking their question. And then... well, I usually realize I just answered the wrong thing because I didn't wait to hear the whole question, or I just realize I completely cut them off. It's definitely something I catch myself doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you often gotten into fights, threatened people, or deliberately broken rules or property?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. I mean, I can be impatient and interrupt people, like I said, but I don't get into fights or threaten anyone. And I definitely don't go around breaking things or deliberately breaking rules. That's just not me.\n\nDoctor: Before age 12, did you also tend to be unusually fidgety or unable to stay seated when expected?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely did. I remember this all starting way before high school, honestly. My elementary school teachers used to write on my report cards that I was bright but always rushed through things. They'd say I talked out of turn a lot, forgot my homework... and my desk was always just stuffed with half-finished papers. So, yeah, I've had a hard time sitting still and focusing for a long time.\n\nDoctor: Besides the interrupting and trouble sitting still, do you also often feel overly active or like you have to keep moving?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel overly active like that. I mean, I get restless, sure, but I don't feel like I have a motor running inside me or anything where I just have to keep moving constantly. It's more just the fidgeting and having a hard time staying put in one spot for too long.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble finishing tasks once you’ve started them?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It happens a lot at work. Like, I'll get most of a sales proposal done, or I'll be updating the CRM system, and... I just leave off the very last step. I'll forget to actually send the final version of the proposal, or I won't log the follow-up in the system, even though I fully intended to finish the whole thing. It just kind of falls off my radar right at the end.\n\nDoctor: Do you also have these kinds of hyperactive or impulsive behaviors outside of work, such as at home or in other settings?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed it being an issue at home, to be honest. I mean, work is definitely where the fidgeting and interrupting happens the most. But when I'm just at my house or out with friends... I don't think I really do those things. At least, it hasn't stood out to me as a problem in those situations.\n\nDoctor: Do you also have trouble sitting still or fidgeting at home or in other places outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, kind of like I just mentioned, it's really just at the office where I get like that. When I'm at home... I can sit on the couch and watch a movie or read just fine. And then... well, I usually realize I just answered the wrong thing because I didn't wait to hear the whole question, or I just realize I completely cut them off. It's definitely something I catch myself doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you often gotten into fights, threatened people, or deliberately broken rules or property?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. I mean, I can be impatient and interrupt people, like I said, but I don't get into fights or threaten anyone. And I definitely don't go around breaking things or deliberately breaking rules. That's just not me.\n\nDoctor: Before age 12, did you also tend to be unusually fidgety or unable to stay seated when expected?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely did. I remember this all starting way before high school, honestly. My elementary school teachers used to write on my report cards that I was bright but always rushed through things. They'd say I talked out of turn a lot, forgot my homework... and my desk was always just stuffed with half-finished papers. So, yeah, I've had a hard time sitting still and focusing for a long time.\n\nDoctor: Besides the interrupting and trouble sitting still, do you also often feel overly active or like you have to keep moving?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel overly active like that. I mean, I get restless, sure, but I don't feel like I have a motor running inside me or anything where I just have to keep moving constantly. It's more just the fidgeting and having a hard time staying put in one spot for too long.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble finishing tasks once you’ve started them?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It happens a lot at work. Like, I'll get most of a sales proposal done, or I'll be updating the CRM system, and... I just leave off the very last step. I'll forget to actually send the final version of the proposal, or I won't log the follow-up in the system, even though I fully intended to finish the whole thing. It just kind of falls off my radar right at the end.\n\nDoctor: Do you also have these kinds of hyperactive or impulsive behaviors outside of work, such as at home or in other settings?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed it being an issue at home, to be honest. I mean, work is definitely where the fidgeting and interrupting happens the most. But when I'm just at my house or out with friends... I don't think I really do those things. At least, it hasn't stood out to me as a problem in those situations.\n\nDoctor: Do you also have trouble sitting still or fidgeting at home or in other places outside of work?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, kind of like I just mentioned, it's really just at the office where I get like that. When I'm at home... I can sit on the couch and watch a movie or read just fine. I haven't noticed any fidgeting or trouble sitting still when I'm off the clock.\n\nDoctor: Do these attention problems, like losing track or not finishing tasks, also happen outside of work, such as at home or in social situations?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean... at home, I'm pretty good about getting things done. If I start cooking or doing laundry or something, I just finish it. And socially, I haven't really noticed any issues with losing track of things when I'm with my friends. It seems like the attention stuff is really just a problem for me when I'm on the clock.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

cut them off.\n- key: S017\n  name: Waiting_Turn_Difficulty\n  description: Often has difficulty waiting his or her turn.\n  manifestation: Waiting my turn on client calls or in office discussions Back in elementary school, my teachers would always write notes on my report cards saying that I was bright, but that I rushed through my work. So, it's definitely been going on for a long time.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or have trouble staying still, even when you need to?\n\nPatient: Yeah, mostly just in the way I mentioned about the meetings. But honestly, I haven't really noticed it too much outside of those long situations at work.\n\nDoctor: When you’re not able to sit still, do you also find yourself blurting things out or interrupting other people?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. It's definitely something I catch myself doing.\n\nDoctor: Have you often gotten into fights, threatened people, or deliberately broken rules or property?\n\nPatient: No, not at all. I mean, I can be impatient and interrupt people, like I said, but I don't get into fights or threaten anyone. And I definitely don't go around breaking things or deliberately breaking rules. That's just not me.\n\nDoctor: Before age 12, did you also tend to be unusually fidgety or unable to stay seated when expected?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely did.
